--- Commit Message ---
LU-20689 utils: Support decimal (SI) size units

llapi_parse_size() took a trailing "B" as decoration, so "1GB"
meant 2^30 while the kernel's own sysfs parser reads it as 10^9,
and a loose suffix such as "1Gijunk" was taken as 1GiB.  Read a
trailing "B" as 10^3-based, keep "G" and "GiB" 2^10-based, and
validate the whole suffix in one helper.

Do so only where the size is not part of a file layout: bandwidth
limits, 'lfs find --blocks' and '--size', ladvise extents, quota
limits, and the copytool and llsom_sync buffers.  A layout size
has to stay a multiple of 64KiB, so "-E", "-S" and "-z" keep
reading a "B" as decoration.

Scaling a fraction as "frac * size_units / frac_d" also overflowed
once the unit was large, so "1.999999999999999999G" came out 1GiB
short.  Scale it after the unit is known, one digit at a time from
the last, and refuse what still does not fit.

Fixes: 1dbf71d8faaa ("LU-7506 lfs: "lfs quota -h" should support petabytes output")
Fixes: 869ea3211d2f ("LU-17263 utils: 'lfs find -blocks' to use 512-byte units")
Fixes: 55968bfabe35 ("LU-13482 utils: bandwidth limit for lfs migrate")
Fixes: be131d125ad1 ("LU-16974 utils: lfs mirror resync to show progress")
Test-Parameters: testlist=sanity,sanity-hsm
Signed-off-by: Xiao Yang <xyang@ddn.com>

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-09-20 23:43:38.000000000) ---
Patch Set 6:

(3 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Looks good; the parsing rewrite holds up. I replayed the new llapi_parse_size() against the old one over ~46k suffix/unit/flag combinations: for flags 0 and 1 nothing that used to be accepted changes value except the fraction-overflow cases, and the only new rejections are the intended ones (strtoull() saturation, a sign hidden in the fraction such as "1.-5G", and junk suffixes). The digit-at-a-time fraction scaling is exact - it produces floor(0.frac * size_units) for every case I tried - and the ULLONG_MAX/10 guard is exactly the bound the loop needs.

One coverage gap worth considering: the compatibility promise the second paragraph of the commit message makes - that "-E", "-S" and "-z" keep reading a trailing "B" as decoration - is not asserted anywhere in 921a-d. A single line in 921a such as `$LFS setstripe -E 1MB -c 1 $f` would pin it down, since 10^6 is not a multiple of 64KiB and the command would start failing the day that changes.

Only optional nits inline otherwise - no need to re-spin for them.

Patch Set 7:

(2 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Looks good. The parser rework holds up: the per-suffix overflow masks are correctly replaced by the single `*size > (ULLONG_MAX - frac_bytes) / *size_units` test, the digit-at-a-time fraction scaling stays below 10 * size_units, and a differential run of the old and new code over ~60 inputs shows only the intended changes (the SI reinterpretation of a trailing B, the tightened suffix tail, and the newly refused overflows). All 21 in-tree call sites were checked; the ones left on the layout path still pass 0, and none of them consume *size or *size_units after a failed parse. Only optional nits inline - no need to re-spin for them.

One thing that is outside the diff: `lfs setstripe -E 1MB` still silently means 1MiB, and lfs-setstripe.1 says nothing about it, while lfs-find.1 now tells the reader that a trailing B means 10^6 for --size/--blocks. Would a sentence in lfs-setstripe.1 (and the -S/-E/-z entries in lfs-find.1) saying that a trailing B is ignored there help keep users out of that trap?
